| TEST345 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_345_a_request_ends_once` | tests/test_in_process_host.py:546 | TEST345: once a request has its terminal, the host sends nothing more for it. Two ways a request ended and more followed: a CANCEL arriving after the handler finished, answered with a second terminal; and a CANCEL while input was open, after which a handler that answers anyway sent its response around the cancel's ERR. The ERR must be that request's only frame. |

| TEST825 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_825_dispatch_request_unconstrained_input` | tests/test_cap_urn.py:1252 | TEST825: a request that leaves its input open has not said what it will send, and is served whatever the candidate takes The open input is unknown — "some input" — not the type "anything": the request is served by exactly the candidates that serve SOME typed call it could become (capdag/formal, serves_unknown_iff). Read as "anything" it was served only by a candidate that takes anything, and asking the fabric "what gives me this?" found nothing. What a candidate cannot do is stand in for the request AS A DESCRIPTION — that relation is typed and composes (Legacy.wildcard_input_not_transitive is why the two were never one). |

| TEST839 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_839_peer_response_delivers_logs_before_stream_start` | tests/test_cartridge_runtime.py:2808 | TEST839: LOG frames arriving BEFORE StreamStart are delivered immediately This tests the critical fix: during a peer call, the peer (e.g., modelcartridge) sends LOG frames for minutes during model download BEFORE sending any data (StreamStart + Chunk). The handler must receive these LOGs in real-time so it can re-emit progress and keep the engine's activity timer alive. Previously, demux_single_stream blocked on awaiting StreamStart before returning PeerResponse, which meant the handler couldn't call recv() until data arrived — causing 120s activity timeouts during long downloads. |

| TEST938 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_938_different_caps_different_hashes` | tests/test_registry.py:230 | TEST938: Two genuinely different caps must hash to different keys. If the canonical-form algorithm ever drifts to coalesce non-equivalent URNs (e.g. by stripping a tag that has functional meaning), this test fails immediately. Renumbered from TEST141 to resolve a collision with Go/ObjC's TEST141 (URL-shape). |
| TEST939 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_939_cap_urn_canonical_form_drops_wildcard_in_out` | tests/test_cap_urn.py:263 | TEST939: The canonical form drops `in=media:` and `out=media:` segments. Every spelling of "the same cap with wildcard in/out" collapses to one byte-identical canonical string. This is the contract that makes registry lookups work: the cap-publisher hashes `<canonical-urn>` to compute the cache key, and every language port (Rust, Go, Python, JS, ObjC) must agree on the canonical form for cross-language lookups to land on the same key. A regression that emitted the wildcard segments would silently move the published cap to a different SHA-256 bucket, 404'ing every reader that hashes the canonical form. |

| TEST975 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_975_single_value_sequence` | tests/test_cbor_util.py:193 | TEST975: split_cbor_sequence works on data that is also a valid CBOR array (single top-level value) |
| TEST976 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_976_wrap_raw_items_roundtrips_and_assemble_rejects_them` | tests/test_cbor_util.py:211 | TEST976: wrap_raw_items_as_cbor_sequence wraps RAW (non-CBOR) item bytes — e.g. PNG frames — into a valid, self-delimiting CBOR sequence that split_cbor_sequence round-trips back to the exact raw items. Regression guard for commit 99df51c4 ("full dag"), which materialised sequence node_data with assemble_cbor_sequence(items) on the *raw* (unwrapped) items from decode_terminal_output. Raw binary is not itself CBOR, so that path failed with "Item 0: ..." (observed as the video extract-frames chain failure). This test asserts the wrap path succeeds AND that the assemble path genuinely rejects the same raw bytes — so the two functions are not interchangeable and the fix is load-bearing. |

| TEST1810 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_1810_media_void_is_atomic` | tests/test_media_urn.py:591 | TEST1810: media:void is atomic — refinements are parse errors. Mirrored across every language port (Rust, Go, Python, Swift/ObjC, JS) under the SAME number. Any divergence is a wire-level inconsistency — the unit type's atomicity is part of the protocol's deepest layer, not a per-port detail. The bare `media:void` parses successfully; any combination with another tag (marker or key=value) MUST fail with VoidNotAtomic. This forecloses a fake taxonomy of unit values; reasons or labels for *why* void is used belong on the cap URN's non-directional tags or in cap args. |

Nothing about that sequence should cost a running cartridge, its warm model, or the work queued on it. |

Forwarding must not silently drop it or substitute a value — a malformed frame is an emitter defect and must surface as one, which is exactly the failure the engine raises for the same frame. |
| TEST1953 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_1953_rule14_streaming_only_on_main_input` | tests/test_validation.py:351 | TEST1953: RULE14 — ``streaming=True`` is accepted on the main input (the stdin arg equivalent to ``in=``) and refused on any other argument: a side option has no wire stream, so it has nothing to consume incrementally, and the rule keeps the executor's hop rule one-dimensional. |
| TEST1954 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_1954_cancel_attribution_roundtrips_in_meta` | tests/test_cbor_io.py:1298 | TEST1954: a Cancel frame is attributed through ``meta`` exactly like an ERR frame — ``code`` / ``attribution_class`` / ``message`` — and every attributed reason round-trips through the codec; the wire carries the class token under the meta key, never a numeric frame key. |
| TEST1955 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_1955_unattributed_cancel_still_cancels_and_close_stream_is_not_a_cancel` | tests/test_cbor_io.py:1322 | TEST1955: a Cancel WITHOUT attribution is still a cancel — it encodes with no meta, decodes as ``CancelReason.unattributed``, and its terminal reads CANCELLED / internal; an unknown class token in meta degrades to "no class", never to a rejected frame (a cancel must always act). A CLOSE_STREAM frame is its own type and is never a cancel. |
| TEST1961 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_1961_cancel_terminal_carries_its_attribution` | tests/test_in_process_host.py:176 | TEST1961: the in-process host answers a Cancel in the cancel's OWN attribution — ERR ABORTED/resource for a host abort (message carries the reason), ERR ABORTED_COLLATERAL with the originating failure's class for collateral, ERR CANCELLED/user for an operator's cancel, and ERR CANCELLED/internal for an UNATTRIBUTED cancel, which still cancels. A CLOSE_STREAM is a no-op for a handler with no live feed: the request continues and completes normally with END. |
| TEST1962 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_1962_a_changed_bundled_cartridge_is_refused_when_started` | tests/test_cartridge_host.py:1615 | TEST1962: a host holds a bundled cartridge to its recorded hash when it starts it, and refuses a changed one by name, without starting it. Discovery no longer hashes a bundled cartridge (TEST1931), so this is the check that catches a changed bundle. The entry point cannot be started: a host that skipped the check would fail at the spawn instead, which is what the unchanged case shows. |

| TEST7114 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_7114_transient_unavailability_does_not_fail_queued_work` | tests/test_request_state.py:413 | TEST7114: a cartridge that disappears and comes back does NOT terminally fail the work queued behind it. This is 17.2's "queued bodies are not assigned terminal failure from another body's process loss; once a replacement instance advertises capacity, subsequent queued work is admitted to that live instance". The regression this pins: a single failed registry-manifest fetch retired three live cartridges for ~24s, and every queued ForEach body was failed with "became unavailable while waiting for capacity" — 195 bodies lost to an outage that had already healed. |

| TEST8111 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_8111_decode_frame_normalizes_narrow_float_widths` | tests/test_cbor_frame.py:1855 | TEST8111: decode_frame reads narrow-width wire floats into the single in-memory float representation, matching the reference decoder (ciborium yields f64 for every CBOR float width). The reference ENCODER shrinks lossless floats to half-precision on the wire (0.5 travels as 0xf9), so a mirror must read a half- or single-precision progress and still expose it numerically. |
| TEST8112 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_8112_half_precision_progress_survives_decode_encode_relay_hop` | tests/test_cbor_frame.py:1877 | TEST8112: the relay forwarding hop — decode a cartridge frame whose progress rode the wire as half-precision, re-encode it, and decode again: the value must survive as a number and the re-encoded bytes must never contain CBOR undefined (0xf7). In the Swift mirror this exact hop corrupted progress to undefined (read as null by the engine), failing every ForEach body on macOS. |
| TEST8113 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_8113_array_split_assemble_normalize_half_precision_floats` | tests/test_cbor_util.py:251 | TEST8113: split_cbor_array / assemble_cbor_array decode-then-re-encode arbitrary CBOR values. The reference encoder shrinks lossless floats to half-precision on the wire, so a ciborium-authored array holding 0.5 arrives as 0xf9 — both directions must carry the value through as a number, never CBOR undefined (the corruption the Swift mirror hit when its CBOR library could not re-encode half-precision). |
| TEST8114 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_8114_straggler_for_terminated_request_is_benign_not_a_drop` | tests/test_relay_switch.py:1766 | TEST8114: A flow frame that CROSSED its request's terminal in flight is a BENIGN straggler — the ordinary teardown race of credit-based flow control must not pollute the routing-anomaly alarm, nor the drop counters at all: the crossing is counted as a straggler, named by frame type. A frame for a RID the table never knew stays a no_route DROP (TEST7025). |

(Mirrors Rust host_runtime TEST8116.) |

| TEST8119 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_8119_coalesce_age_bound_flushes_on_next_write` | tests/test_cartridge_runtime.py:2638 | TEST8119: the coalescing AGE bound — an emission arriving after the buffer's oldest byte crossed COALESCE_MAX_AGE flushes the accumulated batch, so steady token emission lags the wire by at most one write-gap; the tail emitted after that flush ships with close(). | TEST11866 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_11866_piped_stdin_is_read` | tests/test_cartridge_runtime.py:3994 | TEST11866: piped stdin is read, without asking whether it is "ready". Readiness is not the question — existence is. `select` answers the first, is unavailable for pipes on Windows, and reports "not ready" on every platform for a producer that has not written its first byte yet. |
| TEST11867 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_11867_a_terminal_is_not_input` | tests/test_cartridge_runtime.py:4002 | TEST11867: a terminal means nothing was piped. The one case that must NOT read: there is no producer, and reading blocks on a person who is not there to type. |
| TEST11868 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_11868_an_empty_pipe_is_no_input` | tests/test_cartridge_runtime.py:4011 | TEST11868: an empty pipe is no input, not empty input. `cartridge < /dev/null` is a caller who supplied nothing. Returning b"" would fill a required argument with emptiness and take the cap past the check that exists to catch exactly that. |
| TEST11869 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_11869_stdin_is_not_gated_on_select` | tests/test_cartridge_runtime.py:4021 | TEST11869: the reader never consults `select`. The regression guard, and the only one that reproduces the Windows failure on a mac: `select` on a pipe works on POSIX and raises on Windows, so a test that merely checked the RESULT would pass here with the bug still present. What is asserted is that the code path does not go near it. |
| TEST11870 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_11870_a_read_error_surfaces` | tests/test_cartridge_runtime.py:4040 | TEST11870: a read error is raised, not reported as "no input". The blanket `except (OSError, IOError): return None` is what made the Windows bug invisible. A stdin that genuinely cannot be read is a fault to surface — reporting it as "the caller supplied nothing" sends the diagnosis to the caller's command line, which is where a whole day went. |
| TEST11881 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_11881_both_emitters_offer_the_same_surface` | tests/test_cartridge_runtime.py:4081 | TEST11881: the CLI emitter offers everything the streaming emitter does. Found by reflection rather than by a list written here, so a method added to the frame-mode emitter tomorrow fails this until CLI mode has an answer for it — which is exactly what did not happen for `start`. |
| TEST11882 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_11882_cli_start_accepts_what_a_handler_propagates` | tests/test_cartridge_runtime.py:4109 | TEST11882: `start` carries stream metadata without a stream to carry it on. CLI output goes straight to stdout as bytes: there is no envelope for meta to travel in, and nothing is transmitted. That is not the same as having no method — a handler propagating its input's provenance must work in both modes, and in CLI mode the propagation is simply a no-op. |
| TEST11883 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_11883_cli_start_after_output_is_refused` | tests/test_cartridge_runtime.py:4123 | TEST11883: a start after the first emission is refused, in CLI mode too. Frame mode cannot accept one — STREAM_START after a CHUNK is a malformed stream — and a CLI mode that shrugged would let the defect through on the platform it is easiest to test on, to be found in frame mode on another day by somebody else. |
| TEST11884 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_11884_raw_write_also_starts_the_output` | tests/test_cartridge_runtime.py:4142 | TEST11884: writing raw bytes counts as having emitted. `write` is the other path to stdout. A start-ordering check that only knew about `emit_cbor` would accept a start after raw output, which is the same malformed order by a different route. |
| TEST12166 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12166_the_implementation_is_the_proved_model` | tests/test_formal_conformance.py:21 | TEST12166: every row of the proved model's table |
| TEST12368 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12368_a_question_may_leave_a_side_unknown` | tests/test_cap_query.py:30 | TEST12368: "what gives me this, whatever it takes?" and "what can this become?" are questions with a side unknown, and each cap answers with a grade. No cap URN can ask them: `media:` on a side is the type "anything", so a request spelled that way asked for a cap that takes everything, and found none. The unknown side asks nothing; the stated side is held to. |
| TEST12369 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12369_a_querys_tags_are_asked_of_the_tags_a_cap_has` | tests/test_cap_query.py:65 | TEST12369: the cap-tags a query asks for are matched against the tags the cap HAS — a cap's own list is complete. So asking that a tag be absent selects the caps that do not carry it, which no cap needs to declare; and a cap may carry tags nobody asked about. |
| TEST12375 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12375_frame_types_are_the_models` | tests/test_bifaci_conformance.py:111 | TEST12375: every frame type is the type the model means by its number, part of a flow exactly when the model says, and an end exactly when the model says. The runtime's own FrameType is mapped onto the model's by hand; a type mapped to the wrong one would be numbered, ordered and gated as another. |
| TEST12376 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12376_credit_gate_follows_the_model` | tests/test_bifaci_conformance.py:137 | TEST12376: a credit gate answers every acquire, grant and close as the model does, and holds what the model says it holds afterwards. |
| TEST12377 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12377_credit_window_follows_the_model` | tests/test_bifaci_conformance.py:172 | TEST12377: a credit window accepts, refuses and grants as the model does: an arriving chunk is a violation exactly when nothing is left of the window, a grant is due exactly when a batch has built up, a flush grants what is pending, and a continued chunk is granted back at once. |
| TEST12378 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12378_a_zero_window_is_refused` | tests/test_bifaci_conformance.py:209 | TEST12378: the window two ends start with is the smaller proposal, and a proposal of zero is refused — it would deadlock every stream at its first chunk. |
| TEST12379 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12379_a_grant_reaches_the_stream_it_is_for` | tests/test_bifaci_conformance.py:222 | TEST12379: a grant credits the stream it names, or — naming none — the request's only sending stream; a grant that names a stream the request does not have, or names none among several, credits nothing. |
| TEST12380 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12380_reorder_buffer_follows_the_model` | tests/test_bifaci_conformance.py:249 | TEST12380: frames arriving out of order are handed on in the order they were written — each arrival delivers exactly what the model says, holds what it says, and is refused when it says: a number already handed on, one already held, or one more than the buffer may hold. |
| TEST12381 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12381_the_writer_gate_follows_the_model` | tests/test_bifaci_conformance.py:298 | TEST12381: the writer writes exactly the frames the model says: everything until the flow's END or ERR, then nothing of the flow — while credit, which is not of the flow, still passes. What reaches the wire has the flow's numbers 0, 1, 2, … without a gap. |
| TEST12382 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12382_request_table_follows_the_model` | tests/test_bifaci_conformance.py:336 | TEST12382: a request table registers a request once, ends it once, keeps no state for it afterwards, and tells a frame that crossed a request's end from a frame for a request nobody knew — step for step as the model's table does, including when the ring of ended requests is full and the oldest is forgotten. |
| TEST12383 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12383_pool_limits_are_the_models` | tests/test_bifaci_conformance.py:396 | TEST12383: a pool's limit is the smaller of the operator's number and what the cartridge reports, with zero meaning no limit in both and in the result; and a cartridge that is not running is given one request, through `all`. |
| TEST12384 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12384_runtime_pools_follow_the_model` | tests/test_bifaci_conformance.py:431 | TEST12384: the runtime's pools admit, queue and release exactly as the model's scripts say — who holds a slot in which pool, who is in line, who goes next, and how many waiters each pool is holding back — including where a pool's limit rose and a request arrives while somebody in line could go: it waits behind them rather than taking the slot. |
| TEST12385 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12385_what_reaches_the_wire_is_a_flow_in_order` | tests/test_bifaci_conformance.py:520 | TEST12385: what an output stream and the writer put on the wire for one request is a flow in order, by the model's own recognizer: the stream is started once, its chunks are numbered 0, 1, 2, …, its end says how many there were, and nothing of the flow follows END — although a late progress frame and a late chunk were handed to the writer after it. |
| TEST12386 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12386_a_self_report_wakes_the_runtime` | tests/test_bifaci_conformance.py:560 | TEST12386: a handler changing what one of its pools can serve starts whoever in line can now go. Without that a request in line waited for the host's next frame. |
| TEST12387 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12387_switch_admission_follows_the_model` | tests/test_bifaci_conformance.py:633 | TEST12387: the switch admits as the model's scripts say. The main output's stream names no output — it is the cap's answer — and every other stream names the output it is. An output is found by its media URN as a tagged URN; one the cap does not declare is UnknownOutput. |

A stream that names an output yields items tagged with it, and they are not part of the data a caller collects. The response ends at the request's END, not at the first stream's end — it used to stop there, and lose whatever came after — and each stream is credited by name, since a grant that names no stream reaches a callee with several only by guessing. |
| TEST12731 | capdag/capdag-py | python | `test_12731_a_request_stream_that_names_an_output_is_refused` | tests/test_response_outputs.py:566 | TEST12731: a request stream names no output: an argument is identified by its media URN alone.
